SR-Refs
SR-140
Title
The last_approved snapshot: the record of what was blessed
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/baseline_snapshot.py
CodeSymbol
copy_live/load_all/rows_for/exists/stamp/is_drifted/drifted_cells/unanchored_findings
Detail
The approval RECORD, sibling to LLR-158's comparison basis: SR-140 says an acceptance is recorded as a byte-identical copy of the accepted registries riding the approval's own reviewed commit, and that text moved away from that copy is reported regardless of Status movement - this is where the recorded side lives. copy_live(root, seed=False) mirrors seven registries (the four spine tiers plus interfaces/external/components, which carry human-only approval and state cells) BYTE FOR BYTE into docs/archive/last_approved/, preserving repo-relative paths so spine_carrier.resolve/carriers/stem resolve under the snapshot root verbatim, carrier fallback included; the stale other-carrier file for a stem is deleted in the same act, or the next read raises "exists under BOTH carriers". Whole files rather than extracted rows is what makes "snapshot file == live file at the copy commit" DECIDABLE - check_trajectory.staged_snapshot_findings is the mirror invariant that fails a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live on one comparison - and it is also what makes the UNANCHORED rule decidable, because the copy keeps each row's own Status cell, so a live row claiming approval whose snapshot copy reads below it is an approval that never rode a copy. Three refusals are load-bearing and each is the opposite of a convenient degrade: copy_live REFUSES to CREATE the directory without seed=True, because the first snapshot blesses whatever text is in the tree and must ride the owner's reviewed signing commit (reachable only from `intake.py snapshot --seed`); load_all returns None for an absent directory and RAISES on a file that exists and will not parse, because {} and None are opposite claims and the empty one reads as "re-bless everything with no diff shown"; and rows_for is the ONE place the None sentinel is collapsed, so no caller invents its own `or {}`. is_drifted/drifted_cells delegate the cell comparison to LLR-158's split_changed_cells and arm on the RATIFIED half only. stamp() is advisory and derived FROM GIT rather than from a file, so it can never quietly become the ledger this mechanism replaced; every arm degrades to ("", ""). SR-140's two remaining record fields come from the same two places and neither is a cell: the TRANSITION is the copy's Status read against live's (the comparison above), and the ACTING REVIEWER is the AUTHOR of the commit that wrote the copy - which is why the copy must ride the approval commit and why the author is queried from git rather than stamped, since a stamped reviewer is a claim and a commit author is a record. ADVISORY TODAY: unanchored_findings returns strings and joins no failure set - it is promoted to the integrity floor at migration step 7, and not before, because against a pre-seed or pre-rename snapshot it would red every row.
Rationale
The baseline had to leave git history. The derivation it replaces walked the registry for the newest commit at which a row read Verified, which is sound only while every amendment flips its row in the same commit - and the D-9 ladder deletes the flip, so the walk returns HEAD and the diff is empty BY CONSTRUCTION, on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. A snapshot on disk is a baseline OUTSIDE the live file, which is the one thing a walk over the live file can never be. docs/archive/ is the placement rather than an accident: check_vocab.EXEMPT_GLOBS already exempts it, and the snapshot legitimately holds the PREVIOUS vocabulary, so anywhere else reds the vocabulary enforcer on every signing. The design's own argument for whole-file copies is that a human can open the record and `git diff` it - a record only a script can read is a record only a script can audit.
TestRefs
TC-167
Status
Draft

SR-144Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable recordevidence owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-144current content
SR-ID
SR-144
Title
Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable record
SN-Refs
SN-027
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall close a lane that cannot finish into a TERMINAL partial state that merges like any other branch, with one immutable per-close report naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split — refusing a report that declares neither a split nor an explicit deferral, and refusing a second close of the same event rather than overwriting the first.
Rationale
Five successive dedup mechanisms leaked because each reconstructed the return event from a MUTABLE proxy; a document that never moves dissolves the class instead of mitigating it. The refusals are what make "immutable" a property rather than a claim. The split is a field because its absence merged rejected code onto trunk on 2026-08-03: silence about it is different from saying "I could not judge", and only silence is unactionable.
AcceptanceCriteria
A partial close is clean under the real validator (the terminal Deliverable rule is exempt, because an early close leaves the spec's definition byte-identical by design); a terminal spec with no report is LOUD rather than silently un-disposed; a second close is refused and leaves no residue in the lane; a multi-spec close that refuses restores every report it wrote.

LLR-ID
LLR-161
SR-Refs
SR-144
Title
The close ritual and the disposition it owes
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
CodeSymbol
close_partial/render_report/report_path/_restore/_split_lines
Detail
`close_partial` writes the report FIRST (it is the event's identity), refuses when one already exists, and restores EVERY report this call wrote on any refusal — so a multi-spec lane cannot half-close. `intake._close_drafts` titles the disposition on the report PATH and nothing else, and says so LOUDLY when a terminal spec has no report rather than minting zero rows quietly. Every partial and cancelled close earns exactly one disposition row — a re-sweep mints none, a genuinely second close is a second report and so a second row, and a disposition row never mints a disposition for itself (refused at both ends); a complete close is spot-checked at the declared rate rather than gated; continuing partial work drafts a successor carrying explicit lineage that survives the id change, never re-opening a terminal row.
Rationale
The path is the only token in the event's identity that cannot move. An earlier cut folded the report's `claimed_outcome` in as well, which re-read a MUTABLE field on every sweep, so one edit minted a second disposition for the same close — the starvation class returning through a new proxy.

SR-146;SR-144;SR-148
Title
The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
CodeSymbol
compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
Detail
The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
Rationale
Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).

SR-146Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trailevidence owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-146current content
SR-ID
SR-146
Title
Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trail
SN-Refs
SN-005
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
Every prompt the delivered loop launches shall be a shipped, reviewable file with strictly filled slots — listed by digest in a freshness-gated generated catalogue, each session recording which template it used and the fingerprint of what it rendered to.
Rationale
Prose steers the sessions this loop launches and had been reviewable only by reading Python source, which makes the process trusted rather than inspectable. The audit trail is the part that makes the move useful rather than cosmetic: without a per-session digest, "which instruction did this session see" stays an inference. (The argv-vs-shell-string transport decision is design, and lives one tier down in LLR-163.)
AcceptanceCriteria
An unknown or unfilled slot is a refusal; a missing shipped template is a named preflight refusal rather than a first-session crash; the catalogue lists every shipped prompt with its digest and `--check` fails when stale; a line-ending difference is not a digest change; an operator override is named in the session record rather than only in the launch flags.
Priority
S
Verification

Status

LLR LLR-162current content
LLR-ID
LLR-162
SR-Refs
SR-146
Title
Prompts as loaded files, catalogued and fingerprinted
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/prompts.py
CodeSymbol
load/fill/strict_check/digest/catalog_rows/preflight
Detail
`load` reads a shipped template (or an operator override) and raises a named `PromptError`; `fill` refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared slot; `digest` normalizes line endings before hashing; `preflight` turns a missing template into a launch-time refusal. `gen_prompt_catalog.py` renders the catalogue with a `--check` freshness gate, and `agent_common.prompt_fingerprint` records the rendered result per session beside the template's own name.
Rationale
Normalizing before hashing is not cosmetic: the same template checked out CRLF on Windows and LF on POSIX is the same prompt, and a telemetry field that said otherwise would report a model change on every clone.

Title
Argv arrays, and the adjudicator as a routed phase
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_session.py
CodeSymbol
split_cmd/build_argv
Detail
`split_cmd` recognises a JSON array ONLY when the value parses as a list, so no existing template can change meaning; a non-string token raises rather than being stringified. The prompt-transport decision reads the resulting TOKENS, so it is identical for both forms. Alongside it, `ADJUDICATE` joins `agent_route.WEIGHT_PHASES` and `agent_loop.DEFAULT_PHASE_TIER` with its own `route_intent` arm: cross-family like the reviewers, tier pinned by the row's measured estimate, and deliberately deaf to the implementer's escalation overrides.
Rationale
A silent coercion in a registry is a launch that does not match its declaration. And an adjudicator inheriting the implementer's escalation would silently re-tier a judgement because a different row's build went badly.

SR-146
Title
The generated prompt catalogue and its freshness gate
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_prompt_catalog.py
CodeSymbol
render/main
Detail
`render` is PURE — it takes no arguments and reads only the shipped templates — so `--check` and the write path cannot disagree about what "current" means. `--check` compares on NORMALIZED line endings, for the same reason the digest is taken that way: a CRLF checkout is not a stale catalogue. The file is declared in `docs/stack.ini [generated]` as kind `promptcatalog`, so the trunk lane owns it and a work branch never commits it.
Rationale
A generated artifact nobody re-generates is a document that lies, and a lying catalogue is worse than none: it invites the operator to conclude the wrong thing about the session they are debugging. Same contract `gen_arch_map --check` and `gen_skills_index --check` hold.

SR-147One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spineevidence owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-147current content
SR-ID
SR-147
Title
One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spine
SN-Refs
SN-002;SN-012
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The kit shall carry the SN->SR->LLR->TC spine in ONE machine-parseable carrier rather than two (stakeholder needs as markdown prose tables, the other three tiers as CSV, as they were until the 2026-08-10 cutover), and shall provide a mechanical converter that proves the conversion cell-for-cell over the live registries BEFORE the authority flip - refusing, with a nonzero exit that names the row and the field, any conversion in which a cell does not survive the round-trip.
Rationale
The two-carrier split has no recorded rationale and costs on both sides. Reading 32 need rows takes ~166 code lines across 14 functions in 8 modules - six bespoke scanners, two of them F5 twins pinned by nothing but a docstring, which have already drifted once and rendered a phantom root in the dashboard - while reading all 436 SR+LLR+TC rows takes csv.DictReader. CSV also cannot represent the cells that actually exist: five hold a literal pipe, 552 hold commas, the longest is 1,553 characters, and an embedded newline has no representation at all. A structured carrier turns three integrity rules into properties of the parse - a duplicate id becomes a decode error, a ref list becomes a typed array (retiring the split-on-whitespace rule that read `and` as an orphan), and an empty cell becomes an absent key, so `unset` and `set to empty` stop being the same value. The converter is the migration's evidence, not its convenience: SR-129's 140-cell lesson is that an unproven representation change is exactly where a registry silently loses cells. One-shall waiver (13v): the single-carrier claim and its converter-proof are one contract — the converter is the migration's evidence — and the converter's mechanics already decompose to this row's LLR chain; splitting would separate a claim from the proof that makes it checkable.
AcceptanceCriteria
migrate_carrier.py --check over the live registries exits 0 reporting the round-trip clean for every row of every tier; a cell that is dropped, truncated, re-typed or re-ordered is REFUSED with exit 1 and a finding naming the registry, the row id and the field; and the emitted text re-parses with tomllib.
Priority
C
Verification
Test
Status
Planned
Phase
5
Aspect
trajectory
LLR LLR-165current content
LLR-ID
LLR-165
SR-Refs
SR-147
Title
The spine carrier converter (markdown + CSV -> TOML)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/migrate_carrier.py
CodeSymbol
convert/rows_to_toml/sn_to_toml/compare/toml_scalar
Detail
convert() walks the four tiers and returns (findings, written_paths), so the caller decides whether a lossy conversion writes. rows_to_toml emits `[<table>.<ID>]` with the id PREFIX RETAINED and BARE - TOML allows `-` in a bare key - because ~6,400 hand-authored citations use the prefixed token and a numeric key buys no type change (TOML keys are always strings, and `001` and `1` are two legal DIFFERENT keys, which is a near-miss the prefixed form does not have). cell_to_value types by COLUMN, not by guessing: REF_COLS become arrays, INT_COLS become ints, an empty cell becomes an absent key, and everything else stays a string - this is a carrier change, not a schema change. toml_scalar emits multi-line basic strings for anything long or newline-bearing (tomllib is read-only by design, so the kit emits its own, as it already does in wi_convert.toml_string and bootstrap.set_process_key). sn_to_toml keeps the edge-case tier's own lifecycle/scenario/expected fields as THEMSELVES and writes kind= from the heading, retiring section-as-state. compare() re-reads what was emitted and diffs it against an {id: {key: text}} projection built from either carrier, so ONE comparison serves both instead of a second copy that drifts.
Rationale
The round-trip check is the requirement, not a convenience - and a check that cannot fail would be the false green SN-008 forbids, which is why the loss detector is driven against real corruption classes rather than only against a clean run. Ref cells compare on the TOKEN LIST because the conversion re-joins them with the canonical separator and the original spacing was never part of the meaning; comparing raw text there would report a loss that is not one. The edge-case fold that traj_parse._sn_fields performs is a PRESENTATION rule the markdown table forced, so it stays in the exporters - baking it into the carrier would make the exporter's reading the only reading there is.
TestRefs
TC-159
Status
Planned
Component
CMP-006
Phase

LLR LLR-166current content
LLR-ID
LLR-166
SR-Refs
SR-147
Title
The spine carrier's one reader, and the dual-home refusal
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/spine_carrier.py
CodeSymbol
SPINE_TABLE/SPINE_COLUMN/rows_from_toml/rows_from_text/resolve/load
Detail
PURE like trace_text (text in, rows out; no git, no argv) except resolve()'s existence check, because the dual-home refusal is the one rule that must not be duplicated. rows_from_toml presents rows under TODAY'S COLUMN NAMES via SPINE_COLUMN - the exact inverse of migrate_carrier.KEY - so the carrier change is data plus a loader rather than a sixteen-module rename; SPINE_TABLE is keyed on the ID COLUMN, not on a path, because a path carries a carrier suffix and therefore moves. An absent key stays absent rather than becoming "", and a decode error returns None rather than {}. resolve() RAISES when both carriers exist rather than picking by precedence, and load() raises on a carrier that exists and will not parse. carriers() returns BOTH paths so an applicability test spans the cutover.
Rationale
None-vs-{} and absent-vs-empty are the same defect twice: {} says the registry had no rows, which for a baseline read means re-bless everything with no diff and for a gate means nothing to check, so a carrier that cannot be read must be ABSENT rather than empty. The dual-home REFUSAL follows the rule bootstrap --migrate-config already applies to the policy dials: a precedence rule lets a half-finished migration keep working while quietly reading the stale half, so the conversion is never finished and nobody finds out. ONE HOME for the vocabulary is an owner ruling (2026-08-10, repo-lock D-6) amending F5: duplicating a vocabulary fails silently - the copy that has not learned a column returns a row missing that cell, which every consumer reads as "the cell is empty".

SR-148Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what orderevidence owed

Title
Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what order
SN-Refs
SN-025;SN-029;SN-027;SN-006;SN-008;SN-010
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall select the work an unattended run does next from the repository's tracked registries and git history alone — shipping no hand-curated next-work or run-phase pointer surface for any live instruction or executable surface to read, write, validate, generate or link, and deriving from no prose surface and no predefined track — in this order: ready adjudication rows first, as a stable partition applied at admission rather than by renumbering the ruled rank table; then unresolved handback records; then the earliest incomplete spine tier in SN-to-SR-to-LLR-to-TC order; then implementation work after test-case layout is complete — with the eligible set and its order deterministic, an item whose declared safety, policy or plan-mode inputs are missing, undeclared or contradictory failing closed for that item alone, human holds applied only from the declared ratification level, nothing admitted past a human-held stop, and the status surface a session reads generated and freshness-gated rather than hand-copied.
Rationale
Without one precedence rule, the same repository can resume into implementation while returned obligations or prerequisite requirement tiers remain unresolved. That produces work against stale intent and makes repeated resumes select different next actions. A recorded selection class, source record and hold decision provide replayable evidence of the choice. Absorbs SR-141 (census F5, ruled overlap): both rows stated where adjudication ranks at admission and the acceptance texts had already diverged in nuance — one SR per (SN-025, loop selection order) per 13p; SR-141's stable-partition mechanism lands as an LLR under this row, and its id retires under the D-1/D-4 doctrine with the forwarding record in the log. ABSORBS TWO MORE, 2026-08-15, on the same doctrine and the same invariant: the top-down read found SN-025's loop work-selection stated THREE times — this row, SR-153 ("Ready work derives from tracked state") and SR-059 ("Retire next-wi; generated status surface") — with the ordering claim made twice and "no hand-maintained pointer" three times, against 13p's one-SR-per-(need, property) rule. SR-153 contributed the derivation source (tracked registries and git history alone), the determinism of the eligible set and its order, and the per-item fail-closed rule for missing, undeclared or contradictory declared inputs; SR-059 contributed the no-pointer-surface obligation in its strong form (no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links one) and the generated, freshness-gated status surface. Both ids retire; `sn_refs` unions to SN-025 + SN-029 + SN-027 + SN-006 + SN-008 + SN-010 so no need loses its carrier, and every shed clause is token-checked into this row's requirement or acceptance with the map recorded in the log. The alternative — an explicit three-way partition — was rejected for the reason census F5 gave when the same question was asked of SR-141: the partition already existed textually and still produced duplicated acceptance. RESTORED 2026-08-15 (adversarial round 2, cross-family, finding F5): SR-059 required the deletion of the two pointer files in BOTH directions — "fresh scaffold AND migrated repo" — and the merge carried the fresh-scaffold half only, so an adopter upgrade could retain the retired authority files while this row still passed. The migrated-repo/resync half is back in the acceptance; it is the half that matters most, because a fresh scaffold never had the files to keep.
AcceptanceCriteria
A ready adjudication row admits ahead of every other class, the ruled rank table unchanged (--explain still reports it) and non-adjudication rows keeping their relative order; an unresolved handback is selected before spine or implementation work, and processing it either resolves the record or mints a provenance-linked successor exactly once; with no unresolved handback, only the earliest incomplete spine tier is eligible; implementation work is ineligible while any required test case remains unlaid; at human_ratification_through = 0 no spine tier produces a human hold, levels 1 through 4 hold exactly their documented cumulative tiers, and a human-held attestation still drains and surfaces with the adjudication row waiting behind the stop; every selection records the chosen class, source identifier and hold decision. Two resumes over the same tracked state select the same eligible set in the same order; an item with missing or contradictory declared inputs is excluded with a stated reason while disjoint classified work stays eligible; no prose surface participates in the derivation; the ordering and exclusion reasons are exposed for replay. A fresh scaffold ships no next-wi/run-phase pointer and is parallel-by-default; no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links such a pointer; a build-out lane's phase resolves from its branch name with no pointer file; an upgraded or resynced repository that once carried such a pointer carries neither pointer file after the migration and no live dependency on either survives, so the deletion holds for a migrated repository and not only for a fresh scaffold; the status snapshot regenerates mechanically and its freshness is gated.

Title
The adjudication kind: rank-1 slot + no-bar arm + the bar strictness key
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/schedule.py
CodeSymbol
SAFETY_CLASSES/_KIND_CONCURRENCY/_KIND_RANK
Detail
The `adjudication` kind (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A5.2) joins the declared SafetyClass vocabulary: exclusive on the concurrency axis, rank 1 on the order axis - the slot §A1 reserved, filled without renumbering the ruled table. The kind's lane runs NO product bar (integrate._lane_bar_directives + _refresh_bar: the refresh still merges trunk in, runs the trunk step and commits a verified Bar-Green attestation whose summary reads no-bar honestly) because its outputs are Status cells and the work registry, nothing a product bar can speak to; a mixed batch or unreadable frontmatter fails TOWARD the bar. Rationale

Title
Admission order and the red-TC census
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
CodeSymbol
_judgement_first/_admission/red_tc_census/parse_red_tc/_implemented_ids
Detail
`_judgement_first` is a STABLE PARTITION over the frontier, applied inside `_admission` before the surface/dispatch split so it can never move work past a human-held stop. `red_tc_census` rides the existing finding-string seam with a distinct prefix; `_red_tc_line` and `parse_red_tc` sit adjacent because the grammar is a contract between two modules, and `intake` routes on the parser rather than re-splitting prose. red_tc_census names, as its own class, a test case that is not green while every requirement it verifies is cited by a work item recorded done — Verified, Draft and Modified test cases are exempt, an open, partial or cancelled citer makes nothing red, and a test case verifying a mix of claimed and unclaimed targets is not red; each named line routes to an adjudication row whose suggested tier is estimated from measured breadth, never defaulted; the census line's grammar has exactly one reader (parse_red_tc).

SR-Refs
SR-146;SR-144;SR-148
Title
The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
CodeSymbol
compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
Detail
The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
Rationale
Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).

Title
Retired-vocabulary refusal in authored surfaces
SN-Refs
SN-004;SN-010
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The harness shall report every occurrence of a retired process tag in a live authored surface — naming the file and line and the vocabulary that replaces it, at a severity that warns by default and fails under --strict — reporting none in a declared historical, generated or attestation-quoting surface, nor in a line or file marked as a declaration site.
Rationale
Realizes SN-004 (the ladder's vocabulary is the one the project is held to) and SN-010 (docs stay honest). OI-21 made this check a CONDITION of the vocabulary conversion rather than a follow-up, on measured evidence: an earlier sweep removed a retired-tag construct from this repo's own open-items registry and it REGENERATED within days, caught only by an audit. A ~2,500-edit conversion held in place by attention is a conversion that comes undone. The carve-outs are as load-bearing as the rule: history is not rewritten and an attestation is never re-worded, because a swept sign-off makes a signed record claim something was signed that was not.
AcceptanceCriteria
check_vocab.py reports a planted retired tag in a live authored file with its path and line, exits 0 under the default severity and 1 under --strict; reports nothing for a file under any declared carve-out class (archive, the project log, attestation briefs, closed and cancelled work-item specs, generated surfaces) including at arbitrary depth; reports nothing for a line carrying the allow marker or a file carrying the allow-file marker; and reports nothing for the word "gate" used to mean a check that can fail.

Title
Retired-vocabulary enforcer
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_vocab.py
CodeSymbol
scan
Detail
Walks the tree, classifies each file through in_scope (declared carve-out globs for historical, generated and attestation-quoting surfaces, plus a text-suffix filter), and reports one finding per offending LINE - not per tag, since a line naming three tags is one sentence to rewrite. Declaration sites opt out through an explicit in-file marker rather than a heuristic, because a heuristic that can be satisfied by accident is how the vocabulary grows back through the enforcer meant to stop it. Wired into check.py at EVERY bar with --strict added from DevBar-Tests on, mirroring check_trajectory's severity ladder: the surfaces the tags regrow into are authored hardest at the lowest bar.

scan/need_findings/load_allow
Detail
Reads the needs registry through spine_carrier.load_needs (either carrier answers; an unreadable registry REFUSES loudly rather than scanning as empty), and a registry that is PRESENT but yields zero scannable need cells - an empty file, or real rows with no need field - is reported VACUOUS rather than clean, while an absent registry stays the pre-scaffold clean skip and a -000-only scaffold registry stays a blank form (REVIEW-A round 1 drove the hole: at DevBar-Reqs an emptied registry hard-failed nothing else in the harness). The three token classes are data (CLASSES), labelled with SN-033's own vocabulary so a finding names the class the acceptance names. Two scope decisions are deliberate and documented in the docstring: SN-### is NOT a process citation (the SN registry is the stakeholder's own document, so a need pointing at a sibling need keeps the reader at the stakeholder tier - the live SN-025 case), and a single-slash, dot-free token is a path only when it RESOLVES in the scanned tree (docs/archive names a real directory and reports; English either/or pairs like subjective/perceptual resolve to nothing and stay exempt - review round 1 narrowed the original blanket exemption). URL spans - scheme:// and scheme-less www. forms alike - are suppressed whole, so a path-shaped tail inside an external address never reports under any class; the span stops at prose delimiters (comma/semicolon) and the www. form requires a multi-label host, so a genuine token abutting a URL and a local www.assets/logo.png both still report (round 3); a sentence-final token's trailing full stop is stripped before the phrase is judged or reported, so its reviewed exception matches (round 2). One finding per token: a later class re-matching inside an already-reported span is the same phrase to rewrite. The exception list (docs/need-form-allow) requires the ' — <reason>' separator per entry, the literal em-dash with spaces (ALLOW_SEP) - a line authored with a plain hyphen, or with no separator at all, declares nothing, so a malformed entry can only ever fail LOUD, never silence a finding. Wired as check.py's need-form step at every bar with NO --strict promotion, deliberately unlike check_vocab's severity ladder: promotion is an owner ruling, and the wiring says so.
TestRefs
(see TC-164)

Title
Independent review routed across families
SN-Refs
SN-026;SN-024;SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
Before unattended work integrates, the delivered loop content shall obtain each review or critique verdict the declared policy requires from a session that did not author the work — resolved per in-process phase and tier from the delivered agent registry's declared (family x model x tier) rows and only while the declared consent surface that turns managed selection on is present, drawn from a different model family wherever one is configured, degrading only to the documented same-family mode — with every selection logged before launch and a non-converging rework loop escalated through the declared ratification level.
Rationale
Realizes SN-026 (cross-family second opinions — every prior carrier of this contract demoted as routing machinery, so the sn_refs join forced the mint; SR-155 cites SN-026 too, for the planner pair, but only this row states the second opinion itself), SN-024 (an author cannot judge its own output) and SN-006. One row because routing, scheduling, scoring and escalation are one delivered contract — that the second opinion happens, independently, or its absence is documented — whose mechanics are the component details below it. Extended 2026-08-15: SN-026's DECLARATION and CONSENT half — `docs/agents.toml`'s pair-rows and the `docs/agents-enabled` surface whose presence turns managed selection on — had ZERO textual occurrence anywhere in the SR layer while `orphans=0` still held, because an orphan count proves a need has a citing row and never that the row carries the whole need. Managed selection is opt-in by consent rather than by configuration alone: a repository that declares families without dropping the surface has declared a capability, not turned one on, and the row now says so where a check can read it.
AcceptanceCriteria
A declared review policy of N schedules N independent reviewer sessions; the declared (family x model x tier) pair-rows in docs/agents.toml are the only candidate source and docs/agents-enabled is the consent surface — absent, managed selection is off and no row is resolved; present, exactly one row resolves per in-process phase and tier; a configured cross-family draw is preferred and its unavailability degrades to the documented mode, never a silent skip; every selection is logged before launch, never a silent model swap; rework iterates within the declared budget and pages per the declared ratification level on exhaustion.

Title
Contested planning rounds for marked work
SN-Refs

B-05
Requirement
For work declared as contested planning, the delivered loop content shall produce the selected decomposition through a bounded round of two independently authored rival plans — cross-critiqued, mechanically coverage-compared and arbitrated by fresh sessions — committing the selected outcome atomically as registry-valid queued rows and surfacing a non-converging round through the declared ratification level, never falling back to a single uncontested plan silently.
Rationale

One row because the dual-plan round is one delivered capability with one observable outcome class (SELECT or PAGE); its state machine, briefs, adapters and isolation rules are the component tier's.

SR-156Serialized, gated integration of parallel lanesratification owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-156current content
SR-ID
SR-156
Title
Serialized, gated integration of parallel lanes
SN-Refs
SN-027;SN-008;SN-025;SN-010
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall land every lane's finished work through one serial, fail-closed integration seam that runs the declared bar on the composed tree and regenerates the shared authority surfaces only at that seam — draining claiming to a clean merged stop under a declared pause, and recovering from git history alone at any crash boundary — never a half-integrated authoritative state.
Rationale
Realizes SN-027 — fan-out narrows to one serialized gated seam before anything lands, in the need's own words — with SN-008 (the seam is fail-closed), SN-025 (recovery from git alone) and SN-010 (the status surface a human reads regenerates at the seam, never on a lane). One row because serial integration is one delivered contract; the trunk step, pause drain, fragment compilation and verdict-freshness mechanics are its component details.
AcceptanceCriteria
A finished branch reaches the trunk only through the serial integrator with the declared bar green on the composed tree — a red parks the candidate and stops the queue loudly; log fragments and generated artifacts are compiled and regenerated only by the serial trunk actor and never committed on a work branch; a declared pause stops claiming and drains in-flight lanes to a merged stop; a crash at any lifecycle boundary recovers from git history without double-assignment.
Priority
M

Test

SR-148;SR-156
Title
next-wi/run-phase retirement + generated status/run-state
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py
CodeSymbol
REGEN_STEPS/regen
Detail
next-wi/run-phase are gone from every surface; the status snapshot is generated by gen_trajectory --status and re-derived by the trunk lane (trunk_step --regen) after each merge - never hand-copied, never written on a work branch. (The run-state generation half retired with the dispatcher at Phase 5.) "Every surface" is BOTH directions of SR-148's restored deletion clause, and they discharge in different places: a FRESH scaffold never writes either file (bootstrap.py's MAPPING does not carry them), and a MIGRATED repo deletes them under project-trajectory/RESYNC_PACK.md's upgrade recipe, which names both paths in its "delete local reliance on the retired surfaces" step. The migrated half is the one that can silently fail, because it is the only one where the files exist to be kept.

Title
Generated status + run-state
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py
CodeSymbol
REGEN_STEPS/regen

(docs/run-state and its four-valued dispatcher outcome retired with the dispatcher at Phase 5.) The snapshot is an adopted-toolkit output surface (REL-002), not a system-under-development output; a work branch never commits the artifact.
Rationale
Split from the retirement row: removing the old pointer and generating the surfaces that replace it are separable, and the second is what keeps the replacement from rotting the same way.

Title
Local integrator (claim + queue + audit)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/integrate.py

Title
Work-branch lane signal + freshness skip
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check.py
CodeSymbol
_claimed_work_branch/steps
Detail
On a claimed work branch the _TRUNK_FRESHNESS_STEPS set SKIPs with a §5.2 notice, exit code untouched; the plan listing still names every step. The claim signal answers in two stages - docs/work/active/<branch>/ on disk (the fast path), else the branch's own committed history (git log -1 -- docs/work/active/<branch>) - so the closing commit that archives the claim, and every commit after it, keep the skip. No git, a failing git, or an empty answer all read as the trunk lane: a false work-branch positive would switch the freshness gates off on the one branch that owns regenerating them.
Rationale
A false work-branch positive would relax the trunk's freshness bar, so every ambiguity resolves toward the trunk lane.

SR-156
Title
Terminal lane outcomes (handback + quarantine)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
CodeSymbol
close_partial/quarantine/report_path/render_report/_restore/diff_records
Detail
close_partial: commits the lane's work as-is, moves each claimed spec to the TERMINAL partial/ (nothing re-claims it, so nothing strands) and writes ONE IMMUTABLE per-close report under docs/handbacks/ naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split. The report is the close EVENT's identity — immutable so it never moves, unique so a second close is a second document — which is what dissolved the starvation class five mutable-proxy dedup mechanisms died on. A second close is refused rather than overwriting the first, and any refusal restores every report the call wrote (schedule reads queued+blockref as blocked) and the driver cannot claim and return it forever. quarantine: the ruled red arm - reverts the product paths to the merge base and keeps the failing diff as a bar-inert .patch under docs/work/handback/, reading `git diff --name-status -z` as three-field RECORDS so a rename cannot revert the wrong path, checking every git return code, and restoring rather than reporting when a revert step fails. Both leave the branch finished, so integrate.branch_outcomes reads the outcome off the spec's DIRECTORY - complete/=merged, cancelled/=cancelled, any open folder=handback - and every lane ends in a merge.
Rationale
Making the outcome the directory leaves no fourth answer and no state file that could hold one; partial work lands in trunk where a future WI can find it instead of on a branch nobody will look at.

SR-Refs
SR-156
Title
Link-aware spec-move ritual (move + relink indivisible)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/spec_move.py
CodeSymbol
move_spec/expected_relink/archive_dest
Detail
move_spec moves a registry/spec file and keeps every markdown link in the repo true as ONE indivisible operation: the moved file's own relative targets are re-relativised against its new directory, every inbound link whose target resolves to the vacated path is redirected (link text untouched, #fragments carried, external/protocol-relative/bare-anchor targets left alone, root-relative targets excused only in the rebase half, line endings preserved via newline=''), and every rewritten file is staged with the move so the ritual lands as one changeset. integrate.claim and handback.close_partial call it for the claim and close moves; the CLI performs the worker's terminal close move and the dated docs/archive/specs/ archival. expected_relink is the pure oracle integrate._abandoned_claim compares byte-for-byte to excuse a crashed claim's own relink writes without widening its conviction to any .md edit.
Rationale
A bare move strands both link directions and the break surfaces late, on the composed tree (measured: one probe archival took the broken-link count from 4 to 8 - 3 outbound, 1 inbound). Making move+relink one indivisible operation is a constraint that leaves the two-thirds state unrepresentable, rather than a check that detects it after the parallel work is done.

Title
Lane mechanics: worktree, worker subprocess, §A2 refresh subprocess
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/lane.py
CodeSymbol
ensure_worktree/worker_argv/spawn_worker/run_worker/spawn_refresh
Detail
One lane's mechanics extracted from the dispatcher (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4.2): ensure the lane worktree (integrate.lane_worktree, the one home both the worker and the refresh agree on), build the worker argv with the plain launch's session dials forwarded explicitly, launch the worker subprocess (spawn_worker non-blocking for the lane table and dispatch._launch's own default; run_worker its blocking form - a DECLARED SEAM with no in-kit caller, kept for a synchronous single-lane run, and the shape dispatch._launch's worker= injection substitutes), and run the §A2 station refresh as the lane's OWN subprocess (spawn_refresh - the invoker's integrate.py refresh CLI under the harness interpreter) so N lanes' bars overlap instead of queueing at the dispatcher (§A4.3). Reports outcomes, decides none: the tree signal (specs moved out of active/<branch>/) plus exit codes are the whole protocol - no state file, no back-channel.
Rationale
The §A4.2 split keeps the deciding half (dispatch.py) apart from the running half, so at lanes=1 the dispatcher degenerates to the serial loop and the concurrency is a table size, not a second code path.

Title
Dispatch-lock claim rung + the spine batch claim
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/integrate.py
CodeSymbol
_dispatch_lock/claim/_claim_refusal/_abandoned_claim
Detail
The §A4.1 authority flip's integrator half (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4.1): _claim_refusal's safety_class != ordinary arm is DELETED (admission is the dispatcher's decision) and the claim REQUIRES the dispatch lock (agent_common.dispatch_lock_path, out/agent-loop.lock - the lock a live dispatcher holds for its process lifetime), acquired at ONE site (_dispatch_lock, mirroring _slot's one-site discipline for the merge slot's own lock) with the ladder run before it so the clean-trunk rung never refuses over the lock's own file: a hand claim on an idle station works, a hand claim during live lanes is unrepresentable, and the dispatcher's in-process claim passes dispatch_lock_held=True. claim/_claim_refusal/_abandoned_claim/_claim_delta are batch-aware: a spine batch is ONE claim commit moving every batched spec (subject joins the ids; the single-WI subject stays byte-identical), and integrate() takes an optional branches= restriction so the dispatcher merges each lane's branch as its own refresh completes.

Title
The unified trunk-side intake mint + the context block + the ratification-level arms
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/intake.py
CodeSymbol
intake_after_merge/mint_gap_rows/parse_dispositions/next_wi_id/context_block/flip_verified
Detail
The R1/R3 invariant sealed (log.md Decisions 2026-08-01): a WI id is created only by a human trunk commit or this helper - lanes never mint. next_wi_id is max+1 over every spec FILENAME under docs/work/ (active/<branch>/ included). Three triggers, serial by construction: (a) the ratified/routed-cell diff on the merged commit via check_trajectory.staged_spine_amendments -> one adjudication row listing each changed row/cell/before-after (routed traced cells: SN-Refs, Verifies, and LLR SR-Refs per the ruled cell split); (b) a merged spec carrying ## Handback -> the disposition row (outcomes cancel / defer / re-queue with drafted follow-up / surface an open item; NEVER minted for an adjudication row - no recursion, and handback.hand_back refuses the act itself); (c) the dispatcher's gap census -> concrete gap-closure rows, deduped against every existing row. The mint commit mirrors the claim's bookkeeping shape; every derived title is deterministic, so the sweep CLI re-run is idempotent.
Rationale

SR-157Spine and work-registry rules red the harness verdictratification owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-157current content
SR-ID
SR-157
Title
Spine and work-registry rules red the harness verdict
SN-Refs
SN-002;SN-012;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall report every declared spine and work-registry rule violation — broken joins, integrity and schema faults, leftover placeholders, requirement-form findings and work-item coherence contradictions — naming the at-fault row and cell, gating the declared failure set at the declared gate while advisory classes never change the exit code.
Rationale
Realizes SN-002 (the chain is mechanically verified, not asserted — this row is the boundary-level home of the verdict SN-002's acceptance asks for), SN-012 (optional registries and opt-outs cost a non-user nothing) and SN-025 (queue-overlap visibility). One row because the adopter-observable contract is one: registry rules produce named findings at a declared severity; which rules, at which severities, is the component detail the rows below it decompose. Deliberate pair (census F6): the PB-format invariant itself is SR-015's; this row's decomposition carries the checker.
AcceptanceCriteria
Each declared registry rule violation is reported with row-level attribution; the declared strict set exits nonzero at its gate and advisory classes never join the exit code; absent or placeholder-only optional registries and declared opt-outs are vacuous; the declared verification vocabulary (including Critique) is accepted and an unknown value fails. The declaration this is read against is, as the current set: trace.py's `ID_PATTERNS`, `REQUIRED_FIELDS` and `ENUM_FIELDS` (the id shapes, the required cells, and the closed vocabularies -- SR `Verification`, TC `Tier` -- with the IF/CMP entries advisory by their own recorded ruling), the `--strict` / `--strict-integrity` / `--strict-schema` / `--no-placeholders` flag families and the exit contract stated in that module's docstring; trace_text.py's `form_findings` and `provenance_findings` (gating) beside `paraphrase_advisories` and `ac_advisories` (advisory, never the exit code); and check_trajectory.py's work-item rules R-A, R-D, R-E and R-F with `docs/process.toml [checks] trajectory_check` as the declared opt-out. A rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default: an unreported one is a finding against this row, not an amendment to it.

LLR LLR-133current content
LLR-ID
LLR-133
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
Spine stand-alone rule
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
provenance_findings
Detail
provenance_findings flags a WI id or a process-doc citation across the normative cells of all three spine registries, joining the --strict failure set via exit_code. The normative cells are the SR Title/Requirement/Rationale/AcceptanceCriteria, the LLR Title/Detail/Rationale, and the TC Method/Expected/Parameters; pointer columns are out of scope, and each finding names the registry, row and cell. Not a finding: an artifact path, a rubric, a sibling spine id, and a port-script name — the ten declared external ports (check.py, bootstrap.py, agent_loop.py, check_vendored.py, gen_cases.py, gen_release_checklist.py, subagent_gate.py, run_menu.py, integrate.py, trunk_step.py) — the script-name carve-out reaching no wider than that set.
Rationale
A citation is a pointer a later reader may be unable to follow, and the row's reason travels with it: when the work item is closed and archived, the row keeps its claim and loses its justification, which is the failure this catches. Applied to every spine registry rather than the SR alone, because the layer the rule could not originally see held the largest pocket of violations — a narrower scope would guard the cleanest layer and miss the dirtiest.

(see TC)

LLR LLR-134current content
LLR-ID
LLR-134
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
Requirement form rules
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
form_findings
Detail
form_findings applies six rules — one obligation per requirement, no non-obligation modal, no actorless passive, no unfalsifiable term, no open-ended clause, no obligation keyword in an LLR Detail — skipping Draft rows, and joins the --strict failure set via exit_code. The negative set holds: a Draft row, a zero-obligation row, a project's own declared alternate obligation keyword, and a multi-clause AcceptanceCriteria are not findings — stated keyword-free here deliberately, since the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use.
Rationale
A row carrying two obligations reports neither when one fails, and undecidable wording is signed on impression rather than evidence — that is the failure all six rules exist to prevent. Draft is skipped because a Draft row is exempt from the child-completeness rules elsewhere in this harness and is by convention the state a requirement occupies while still being written, so an unfinished acceptance criterion is expected there rather than defective; flagging it would make the state unusable for the drafting it exists to allow. This cell says "obligation keyword" rather than quoting the keyword itself because the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use, and an exemption a checker cannot see is one an author can reach for.
TestRefs
(see TC)

Modified
Component
CMP-006

LLR LLR-135current content
LLR-ID
LLR-135
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
Paraphrase advisory
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
paraphrase_advisories
Detail
paraphrase_advisories compares a child cell's significant words (alphabetic runs of four characters or more, lowercased) against its parent's and reports when the share of the CHILD's words that also appear in the parent exceeds the declared threshold — the child is the denominator, so a long parent cannot dilute a short echoing child. Reported in its own advisory pipe, which never joins a failure set. The two watched relationships are an SR Rationale against its own Requirement and an LLR Detail against its parent SR Requirement — the pairs where the parent is unambiguous; each finding emits a stdout WARNING plus a report line with the exit code unchanged.
Rationale
A child that re-words its parent adds a second home for one fact and no decomposition, so a later amendment updates one copy and the two disagree with nothing to report it. The advisory has its own pipe rather than sharing the acceptance-criteria counter because a shared count would report 'ac-advisories' about a finding that is not one, and a reader acts on the counter name.
TestRefs
(see TC)

LLR LLR-160current content
LLR-ID
LLR-160
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
The mechanical queue-overlap pre-filter
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
queue_conflict_findings/_title_tokens
Detail
Three signals over OPEN rows only — Jaccard over title token sets at a deliberately high bar, shared `SR-Refs`, and a shared anchor-stripped `SpecRef` — each reported once per pair per signal with both ids, in sorted order. Printed on the warn-only line beside backlog staleness; never appended to `errors`. A shared stopword alone is never a signal, and the findings stay out of the exit code even under --strict.
Rationale
A low similarity bar turns a useful warn into noise that gets ignored, which is worse than not having the warn: this rung catches the same job minted twice, not two rows in the same area.

SR SR-158current content
SR-ID
SR-158
Title
Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tier
SN-Refs
SN-010;SN-008
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall report a documentation surface that has drifted from what it claims — a broken intra-repo link, a missing vision declaration, a reference to an absent path or symbol, a stale generated doc, or a declared figure without its provenance — at the declared warn-or-gate severity for each class.
Rationale
Realizes SN-010 (navigable, honest documentation) and SN-008 (a green never hides a stale claim). One row because the delivered contract is one honesty verdict over the documentation surfaces; the individual checkers and their carve-outs are component details.
AcceptanceCriteria
A dead intra-repo link, an absent PROJECT-VISION tag, a stale generated doc under --stale, a dangling backticked path or sym: reference, and a declared fig: marker missing cmd/rev each produce a finding at their declared tier; the declared carve-outs (generated blocks, path-ok and fig-ok lines, unmarked figures, URLs and placeholders) stay silent. The tier for each class is read off its declaration site, as the current set: check.py's built-in `doc-navigability` step, which runs check_docs.py from DevBar-Reqs on -- a broken intra-repo link and a missing or repeated PROJECT-VISION tag exit nonzero, orphans warn unless --strict-orphans, and --stale is warn-only; docs/stack.ini's `[step:doc-refs]` and `[step:figures]` rows, which declare `gates = DevBar-Release` for check_doc_refs.py --strict (gating on dangling alone, with untraced counted and never gating) and check_figures.py --strict; and the carve-out surfaces those checkers read -- `docs/orphans-allow`, `docs/declared-absences`, and the path-ok / fig-ok line markers. A class whose severity is stated at none of those sites is undeclared, and this row is unsatisfied until it is declared there.

The SN carrier schema declares the scope field; the checker reports a missing or invalid value naming the row; example -000 rows are exempt; every non-example row in the shipped registry and in this repository's filled one carries a valid value.

SR-165The component partition carries a reproducible derivation recordratification owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-165current content
SR-ID
SR-165
Title
The component partition carries a reproducible derivation record
SN-Refs
SN-040
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered planning content shall leave, with every component partition it produces, a machine-readable derivation record in the tracked component registry rather than session prose — the candidate partitions considered, the declared objective and constraints they were scored against, each candidate's score, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it — such that a live component row whose record omits any of those elements, or a selection the recorded scores do not rank first without a recorded human override, is a reportable finding.
Rationale
Realizes SN-040 — a free-form partition leaves no record of the alternatives weighed, so the next reviser cannot tell load-bearing boundaries from accidents; the record is what lets the choice be re-examined. SN-040 had no citing SR, even as the P5 partition work performed exactly this record. Reproducibility is stated as a recomputation from the record's own declared inputs, not as a reviewer re-running a model session, because only the former can fail — the SR-161 form applied to the partition instead of the perspective set. B-05 alone: the human ruling is an element the record must carry, not an observable the system states at the authority input, which is SR-139's and SR-140's ground.
AcceptanceCriteria
A partition round writes the derivation record into the tracked component registry, never into session prose or a plan document alone; a live component row with no record, or a record omitting the candidate partitions, the declared objective, the constraints, any candidate's score, the selected partition or the human ruling, is reported naming the missing element; a selected partition the recorded scores do not rank first is reported unless the record carries the human override that chose it; the recorded ranking recomputes from the record's own objective, constraints and scores alone.
Priority
S
Verification
Test
Status
Draft
Phase
5
Aspect
process
LLR LLR-172current content
LLR-ID
LLR-172
SR-Refs
SR-165
Title
Component derivation record: element completeness + the ranking recompute
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace.py
CodeSymbol
component_findings
Detail
Extends the existing component pass — the one home that already reads docs/requirements/components.toml and already emits CMP findings — with the derivation-record rules SR-165 states, rather than adding a second reader of the same registry. Per live CMP row: the record must declare the candidate partitions considered, the objective and the constraints they were scored against, a score per candidate, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it; a missing element is one finding naming the row AND the element. Two derived rules on top of presence, because presence alone is satisfiable by a record that says nothing: (1) THE RANKING RECOMPUTES — re-rank the recorded candidates from the record's own objective, constraints and scores and compare with the recorded order, a mismatch being a finding, which is what makes the record falsifiable without a reviewer re-running a model session; (2) A SELECTION THE SCORES DO NOT RANK FIRST is a finding unless the record carries a human override naming that choice, so a legitimate "the scores said A, the human chose B" is expressible and an unexplained one is not. Vacuous by construction on a project declaring no components, and on the example -000 rows. NOT BUILT YET: the record cells do not exist on the CMP schema today, which is why this row and TC-166 are Draft — SR-165 was flipped Inspection -> Test at act 7 precisely because the check is mechanizable, and this is the debt that flip incurred, recorded rather than silently owed.
Rationale
The check belongs where component_findings already is for the reason the census keeps applying: a second module reading components.toml would be a second answer to "is this partition recorded", and the two would drift. The recompute is the load-bearing half — an element-presence check passes on a record whose scores contradict its own conclusion, which is the exact failure SN-040 exists to catch (the next reviser cannot tell load-bearing boundaries from accidents). The override arm is what stops the recompute rule from forbidding human judgement: the requirement is that a departure from the scores is RECORDED, never that it is disallowed.
TestRefs
TC-166
Status
Draft
Component
CMP-006
Phase

TC TC-166current content
TC-ID
TC-166
Verifies
SR-165;LLR-172
Level
Integration
Method
Run trace.py's component pass over fixture component registries: (a) a complete derivation record passes silently; (b) a record omitting each element in turn — candidate partitions, objective, constraints, any candidate's score, the selected partition, the human ruling — is reported once per omission, naming the row and the missing element; (c) a record whose recorded ranking does not recompute from its own objective, constraints and scores is reported; (d) a selected partition the recorded scores do not rank first is reported, and the SAME record with a recorded human override naming that choice is silent; (e) a project declaring no components, and the shipped example -000 rows, are vacuous.

SR-166The delivered package materializes where its manifest declares it willratification owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-166current content
SR-ID
SR-166
Title
The delivered package materializes where its manifest declares it will
SN-Refs
SN-001;SN-007
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered package shall be consumable at its own declared shipped-file manifest — every file the manifest names materialized at its declared destination in a fresh scaffold, and this repository's own instance carrying the structure of the template it ships — with a declared destination a fresh scaffold does not carry, and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's instance, each failing rather than degrading the delivered package silently.
Rationale
Realizes SN-001 (an adopting team gets a working, gated, requirement-traced process without hand-building the tooling — which presupposes that what arrives is whole) and SN-007 (the kit is held to the standard it ships). §1R.6 names this as B-05's own artifact-shaped observable — the package "exists, is complete, and is consumable" — and records that its manifest is already pinned; the 2026-08 re-tier campaign minted fifteen rows and not this one, leaving the crossing the frame describes at greatest length with no requirement stating it, and MAPPING surviving only in SR-163's rationale. Verification evidence is named rather than owed: tests/test_bootstrap.py drives bootstrap.py's MAPPING against the file lists a fresh scaffold must carry, and tests/test_dogfood_sync.py pins this repository's own instance against the template it ships, so the manifest cannot declare one thing and the package deliver another. Deliberately distinct from its two neighbours: SR-163 asks why each shipped file EXISTS (SN-038, purpose coverage), SR-010 asks whether the generated scaffold runs GREEN, and this row asks whether what was promised ARRIVES WHERE IT WAS PROMISED. NARROWED 2026-08-15 (adversarial round 2, cross-family, finding F7, convergent with the second read's H1): as minted, this row ALSO required that a named source the package does not carry, and a shipped file the manifest does not name, each FAIL — and SR-163 already reports both classes under a configurable warn-versus-gate policy. Two rows prescribing different outcomes for one observable is a contradiction an implementer can only resolve by violating one of them, so the two manifest-PRESENCE clauses are shed here and referenced to SR-163; each was token-checked into SR-163's requirement ("missing files, stale inventory entries") and acceptance ("a shipped file absent from it", "a stale entry naming a file that no longer ships") before deletion, so no obligation vanished — only its second, conflicting statement. What is left is what SR-163 does not ask: not whether a file is accounted for, but whether it LANDS — at its declared destination in a fresh scaffold, and in this repository's own instance of the template it ships.
AcceptanceCriteria
A manifest entry whose destination a fresh scaffold does not contain fails naming the destination; and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's own instance fails naming the surface. Evidence: tests/test_bootstrap.py (the manifest's destinations against the fresh-scaffold file lists) and tests/test_dogfood_sync.py (shipped template against this repository's instance). Manifest PRESENCE is deliberately NOT restated here — a named source the package does not carry, and a shipped file the manifest does not name, are both SR-163's, reported under its declared warn-versus-gate policy.
